Tanidis, M. Viel

**Abstract:** Wide-area cluster catalogues from Stage-IV surveys will substantially improve the precision of cluster clustering measurements, making the control of observational systematic effects essential for cosmological inference. In preparation for the first Euclid data release (DR1), we assess the impact of the survey mask and non-homogeneous sky coverage on the two-point correlation function of galaxy clusters and its covariance. Using DR1-like lightcone mocks, we confirm that the irregular footprint does not introduce appreciable changes in the two-point correlation function when the same mask is applied to the random catalogue. We also find that spatial inhomogeneities, implemented as variations of the cluster selection function in different patches of the sky, do not significantly bias the clustering amplitude, up to levels of variation well above those expected for DR1. When the degree of inhomogeneity is increased to the regime where the effect becomes significant, we show that its amplitude is related to the maximum variation of the selection function among different regions, rather than to the number of such regions. We further find that irregular mask geometry and non-uniform sky coverage both increase the clustering covariance, introducing small, nearly scale-independent offsets that can be captured by nuisance parameters in the covariance model. Our results consolidate the treatment of mask systematic errors for DR1 and inform the methodological refinements required for future \Euclid cluster cosmology.

## 45. [Gaia DR3 Reveals the Kinematics of Young Stellar Objects in the Carina Nebula: Peripheral Expansion and Central Rotation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06488) <a id="link45"></a>

**Authors:** Xinyu Yang, Yuxin He, Chaojie Hao, Yiwei Dong, Y. Xu, Yingjie Li, ZeHao Lin, DeJian Liu, Jarken Esimbek, Jianjun Zhou, Gang Wu, Xindi Tang, Dalei Li

**Abstract:** We investigate the kinematics of the Carina Nebula using a sample of young stellar objects (YSOs) cross-matched with Gaia DR3, together with $^{12}\mathrm{CO}\,(1\mbox{--}0)$ molecular gas observations and APOGEE radial-velocity data. The results show that the proper motions of the YSOs in the four peripheral subregions of Carina display a systematic outward expansion, and that the two-dimensional expansion velocities of the subregions are approximately $0.57\mbox{--}4.56~\mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$. The YSOs in the Central Region exhibit ordered rotation around an axis at a position angle of $86.9\pm1.5^\circ$, with a rotation velocity of $1.75\pm0.03~\mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$. Further momentum estimates suggest that the current expansion in the peripheral regions may result from the combined effects of feedback from massive OB stars and possible past supernova explosions. These results indicate that feedback from massive stars plays a dominant role in the dynamical evolution of the giant molecular cloud in the Carina Nebula.

## 46. [The Environment of High-Energy Transient Host Galaxies at High Redshifts as Observed by SHARP](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06352) <a id="link46"></a>

**Authors:** L. Izzo

**Abstract:** The study of transient events, such as Gamma-Ray Bursts (GRBs) and extreme Supernovae (SNe), provides a unique probe of star-forming environments in the early Universe. Traditional global host galaxy studies often fail to capture the local conditions around the progenitor, leading to potential mischaracterizations due to metallicity gradients and spatial inhomogeneities. Using high spatial resolution integral field unit (IFU) data, such as those obtained with SHARP/VESPER, allows us to spatially resolve the host galaxies and accurately determine the physical properties (metallicity, stellar age, kinematics) at the precise explosion site. Here, we review the need for near-infrared spatially-resolved observations to link progenitor properties, such as the required low metallicity for long GRBs, to their birth environments, and highlight the crucial role of instruments like SHARP in advancing high-redshift transient science, including the characterization of SNe without associated GRBs and Kilonovae hosts.

## 47. [How sky subtraction scales affect Intracluster light measurements](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05467) <a id="link47"></a>

**Abstract:** Deep imaging surveys are opening a new window on the low surface brightness universe. However, the recovery of diffuse emission depends on the survey depth and how the sky background is treated. Estimating the sky on scales comparable to astrophysical sources risks biasing flux measurements. This effect is particularly significant for intracluster light, the most extended diffuse baryonic component of galaxy clusters. We show in a $M_{500}$--$z$ plane that the sky subtraction scales employed in the Legacy Survey of Space and Time Data Preview 2 and Hyper Suprime-Cam Public Data Release 3 (HSC-PDR3) impact clusters with $M_{500}\gtrsim10^{13.4}\,\mathrm{M_\odot}$ at all redshifts, whereas HSC-PDR2 only oversubtracts the most massive systems at $z < 0.1$. The larger sky subtraction scale anticipated for the full LSST survey will mitigate this bias.

## 48. [Inflationary attractors in metric-affine gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06750) <a id="link48"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.06750

**Authors:** Ilaria Andrei, Christian Dioguardi

**Abstract:** We study single-field slow-roll inflation in metric-affine gravity (MAG). We consider the most general action up to operators linear in curvature and quadratic in the distortion, without propagating torsion and non-metricity. We first impose projective invariance and promote every coefficient to a function of the scalar field. After integrating out the non-dynamical distortion, the net contribution is a correction $k(\phi)$ to the scalar kinetic term. We then consider the class of $\xi$-attractors for which the non-minimal couplings and the scalar potential are fixed by a single function $f(\phi)$. It is well-known that in the metric formulation the strong-coupling regime is universally the Starobinsky attractor. In the Palatini framework, instead, this attractor is lost and the predictions become model-dependent. Here, we show that the MAG framework restores universality: in the strong coupling limit with finite ratios of the distortion couplings to $\xi$ we have an $\alpha$-attractor, $n_s=1-2/N_*$ and $r=12\alpha/N_*^2$, with $\alpha=1+\zeta/(6\xi)$. The ratio $\zeta/\xi$ encodes the distortion contribution. Starobinsky and Palatini inflation are recovered as specific limits. Through a numerical parameter scan we show that almost all the healthy points lie on one of the two attractors : either Nieh-Yan-like inflation (studied in a previous work and extended here) or the new MAG $\alpha$-attractor found here. Finally, we explicitly drop projective invariance and analyse the general MAG theory. The same large-field asymptotic structure survives. We conclude that no extra inflationary phenomenology is reached by the non-projectively invariant operators: the $\alpha$-attractor is a general prediction of MAG.

**Relevance:** 1
**Novelty:** 6

## 49. [Gravitational-Wave Constraints on $R^2$ Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06902) <a id="link49"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.06902

**Authors:** Pawan Kumar Gupta, Laura Sagunski

**Abstract:** Gravitational waves from compact binary coalescences provide a unique opportunity to test general relativity in the strong-field regime. Quadratic $f(R)=R+aR^2$ gravity is among the simplest higher-curvature extensions of general relativity and introduces a massive scalar degree of freedom whose finite interaction range can modify compact-binary dynamics and gravitational-wave emission. We map this theory onto the effective-field-theory waveform for generic massive scalar-tensor gravity, obtaining a theory-specific inspiral phase correction governed by the single coupling parameter $a$. Using Bayesian analyses of the binary-neutron-star events GW170817 and GW190425, we obtain a combined constraint on the quadratic-curvature coupling of $a\lesssim6.2\times10^6\,\mathrm{m}^2$ at $90\%$ credibility, corresponding to a lower bound on the scalar mass of $m_s\gtrsim3.2\times10^{-11}\,\mathrm{eV}$. This constitutes the strongest astrophysical constraint on the quadratic-curvature coupling, improving the previous best bound by approximately five orders of magnitude and demonstrating the sensitivity of binary-neutron-star inspirals to finite-range scalar interactions in the strong-field regime.

## 50. [Observational Test of the Maximum-Entropy Conjecture for Black-Hole Mergers with GW250114](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05718) <a id="link50"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.05718

**Authors:** Yang Jiang, Qing-Guo Huang

**Abstract:** The remnants of binary black-hole (BBH) mergers are accurately described by numerical relativity, but the physical principle governing the selection of the final states remains unclear. A recently proposed maximum-entropy conjecture suggests that a BBH merger produces the Kerr BH that maximizes the Bekenstein-Hawking entropy, subject to the post-Newtonian formalism or balance laws for energy and angular momentum. In this work, we test this conjecture with the event GW250114, inferring the progenitor and remnant properties from the observed pre-merge and post-merge signals, separately. We construct the surrogate Kerr entropy associated with candidate remnant states along the inferred energy-angular-momentum trajectory. We then compare the maximum-entropy state with the independently inferred mass and dimensionless spin of the final BH. We find that the conjecture predicts a remnant mass of $M_f=70.0^{+0.35}_{-0.21}\,M_\odot$ and spin of $\chi_f=0.70^{+0.00}_{-0.01}$, compared with the ringdown estimates $M_f={72.3}^{+3.6}_{-3.7}\,M_\odot$ and $\chi_f=0.74^{+0.05}_{-0.07}$. The entropy deviation is consistent with zero within the $68\%$ credible interval for our fiducial analysis, and variations in the pre-merger truncation time and quasi-normal modes (QNM) content reveal no compelling discrepancy with the conjecture.

## 51. [Near-Quantum-Limited Readout for the Princeton Axion Search using a sub-GHz Kinetic Inductance Traveling Wave Parametric Amplifier](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06761) <a id="link51"></a>

**Authors:** Joe Wiedemann, Roman Kolevatov, Jianyang Fu, Joelle-Marie Begin, Nate Otto, Felix St-Amour, Lyman Page, Henry LeDuc, Peter Day, Saptarshi Chaudhuri

**Abstract:** Parametric amplifiers are a workhorse for near-quantum-limited measurements above 1 GHz. However, amplifier development has been more limited below 1 GHz. We present a kinetic inductance traveling-wave parametric amplifier (KI-TWPA) operating over 300-700 MHz, with gain exceeding 30 dB and pump powers of a few hundred nanowatts. The system noise performance, as measured by a variable-temperature noise source, is 2.5-3.5 quanta. This device is a versatile new tool for precision measurements of sub-GHz signals, with applications including readout of semiconductor spin qubits, superconducting detector arrays, and searches for new physics beyond the Standard Model. We characterize aspects of the amplifier performance relevant to axion searches, including gain drift, compression point, amplifier noise at various operating temperatures, readout with an input circulator, and magnetic field insensitivity.

## 52. [Characterizing Gravitational Wave Electromagnetic Counterparts with the Schmidt Observatory System](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05461) <a id="link52"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.05461

**Authors:** Keerthi Kunnumkai, Antonella Palmese, Thomas Wevers, Mattia Bulla, Suvi Gezari, Saul Perlmutter, Liliana Rivera Sandoval, Gudmundur Stefansson, Mickael Rigault, Arpita Roy

**Abstract:** The emergence of gravitational wave (GW) multimessenger astronomy, marked by the detection of GW170817 and its associated kilonova, has opened a new window into the physics of compact binary mergers. Building a statistical population of kilonovae remains a challenge, limited by the low neutron star merger detection rates and the rapid evolution of these faint transients. The former problem will be overcome with upcoming LIGO/Virgo/KAGRA (LVK) sensitivity improvements leading to the fifth Observing Run (O5), while the latter will be addressed by the Schmidt Observatory System (including the Argus Array, the Deep Synoptic Array (DSA), and the Lazuli Space Observatory), in addition to ongoing and upcoming surveys (including the Rubin Observatory Legacy Survey of Space and Time). Using simulations of expected kilonovae at LVK O5 sensitivity, we find that Argus and Rubin could detect binary neutron star kilonovae at a rate of 0.3-4.2 yr$^{-1}$ and 0.6-7.0 yr$^{-1}$ respectively. For neutron star-black hole mergers, Rubin could detect up to 1 kilonova per year. Lazuli will be able to characterize this kilonova population down to $g \sim 26$ mag as early as 12 hours post merger. Additionally, $\sim35\%$ of these events are expected to exhibit a jet afterglow that will be detectable by DSA. We further characterize kilonova spectra to determine observable features of heavy element production. Finally, we develop an observing strategy that combines high cadence imaging and spectral monitoring, maximizing Lazuli's ability to constrain ejecta properties and composition of GW counterparts with reasonable observing time.

## 54. [The Cosmology of Hawking Axions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05747) <a id="link54"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.05747

**Authors:** Abdelaziz Hussein, Gordan Krnjaic, Sarunas Verner

**Abstract:** Since primordial black holes (PBH) are always non-relativistic, their cosmological energy fraction grows relative to that of radiation. Thus, for even a tiny initial PBH abundance, the universe generically enters an epoch of early matter domination, which ends when the PBH evaporate to restore the hot big bang. We consider the possibility that a PBH dominated era yields a population of boosted axion-like particles (ALPs) produced from Hawking evaporation. Eventually these ``Hawking axions" become non-relativistic and decay to photons, which can affect various cosmological and astrophysical observables. Here we place new constraints on these particles through their impact on big bang nucleosynthesis, cosmic microwave background, large-scale structure, and X-ray observables. Furthermore, if Hawking axions remain relativistic through recombination, then depending on the progenitor PBH mass, they contribute $\Delta N_{\rm eff}\simeq0.05$-$0.11$, which may be observable with future CMB probes.

## 55. [The kinematic Kelvin-Helmholtz instability-driven dynamo: implications for merging compact objects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05880) <a id="link55"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.05880

**Authors:** James R. Beattie, Amitava Bhattacharjee, Elias R. Most, Bart Ripperda, Alexander Philippov, Ralf S. Klessen, Salvatore Cielo

**Abstract:** Binary neutron star (BNS) mergers excite a Kelvin--Helmholtz instability (KHI) in the shear layer between them. It is parasitized upon by the magnetic field to form a powerful magnetic dynamo. Using a large ensemble of high-resolution visco-resistive KHI-driven dynamo simulations we construct analytical, asymptotic in Reynolds number ($\rm{Re}$) models for the magnetic field growth rates and mean vertical field dynamo onset times. Our models suggest that in the $\rm{Re} \gg 1$ limit, the mean vertical field is seeded immediately, kinematic growth rates become asymptotic in $\rm{Re}$, and the dynamo moves out of the kinematic stage on $\mathcal{O}(0.1\,\rm{ms})$ timescales, readily attaining magnetar-strength fields during this transition. This suggests that most global BNS merger simulations are not time-resolving the correct KHI-driven dynamo regime.

## 56. [The M87 Black Hole Mass from JWST and Keck Stellar Kinematics Spanning 10 pc to 10 kpc](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05486) <a id="link56"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.05486

**Authors:** Emily R. Liepold, Chung-Pei Ma, Refa M. Al-Amri, Jonelle L. Walsh

**Abstract:** We combine JWST NIRSpec and Keck KCWI observations to construct a contiguous 2D map of M87's stellar kinematics spanning three decades in projected radius, from ${\sim}10$ pc to ${\sim}10$ kpc, and measure the mass of its central supermassive black hole. The innermost stellar kinematic measurements probe projected radii as small as ${\sim}3$% of the black hole's sphere of influence radius, or approximately 30,000 Schwarzschild radii. The NIRSpec spectra within $R < 0.45''$ are contaminated by quasiperiodic "wiggle" artifacts. We develop hyphen, which uses detector spectral traces to predict how the wiggle frequency varies with wavelength, then fits and subtracts the wiggle pattern from each spectrum in each exposure before combining the data. The cleaned spectra reveal a pronounced central rise in stellar velocity dispersion and $h_4$. Triaxial Schwarzschild stellar orbit modeling with the TriOS code, fitting ${\sim}$5,000 kinematic constraints across 618 spatial bins from JWST and Keck, yields a black hole mass of $M_{\mathrm{BH}}=(5.38 \pm 0.13\pm 0.22)\times10^9\,M_\odot$. This result is consistent with our prior KCWI-only determination but with the statistical uncertainty reduced by a factor of 2-3, now subdominant to the systematic uncertainty in the distance ($D$) to M87. Our inferred mass-to-distance ratio is $\theta_g=GM_{\mathrm{BH}}/(c^2 D) = 3.16 \pm 0.08\, \mu$as, whose 90% credible interval overlaps with that obtained from the multi-epoch Event Horizon Telescope (EHT) analysis, providing a robust and independent prior for future EHT studies.

## 57. [Spacetime Evolution Induced by a Massive Cosmic String in Numerical Relativity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05996) <a id="link57"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.05996

**Authors:** Yoshihiro Kishimoto, Cristian Joana, Teruaki Suyama

**Abstract:** Cosmic strings are one-dimensional topological defects formed during phase transitions in the early universe. ''Massive'' cosmic strings possess a sufficiently large tension to close their transverse space, forming a conical singularity outside the string, as a static gravitational effect. In a dynamical context, such strings are expected to drive topological inflation. In this work, we investigate the spacetime evolution induced by the formation of a massive cosmic string in a radiation-dominated cosmological background, imposing cylindrical symmetry. Using full numerical relativity simulations, we observe that the string core undergoes topological inflation and that its boundary moves inwards superluminally, which causally disconnects the core from the outer universe. Between the core and the asymptotic regions, a nearly matter-dominated decelerating region originates sourced by the oscillating string fields. This region is bounded by a local maximum (''belly'') and local minimum (''throat'') of the circumferential radius, while the throat recedes outwards at about 10% the speed of light. Furthermore, a gravitational wavefront is emitted from the string, characterizing the light cone of the string formation. Even behind the wavefront, the exterior region no longer exhibits a ''conical'' structure. Based on the simulation results, we construct a Penrose diagram, reflecting the causal structure of the entire spacetime.

**Novelty:** 5

## 58. [A high-frequency turnover in the persistent radio source of FRB20201124A](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05694) <a id="link58"></a>

**Authors:** G. Bruni, G. Gianfagna, L. Piro, A. L. Thakur, L. Nicastro, E. Palazzi, A. Rossi, S. Savaglio, A. Gardini, Y. -P. Yang, B. Zhang

**Abstract:** Persistent radio sources (PRSs) associated with repeating fast radio bursts probe the immediate magneto-ionic environment of their engine and thereby constrain the nature and evolutionary stage of the progenitor. The persistent radio source of FRB20201124A is the most extreme in the current sample, with a spectrum that remains flat or inverted up to 22 GHz. We aim to constrain the centimetre-to-millimetre spectral energy distribution of this source and to test whether its properties are consistent with a young, rotation-powered magnetar wind nebula. We analysed archival ALMA Band 3 observations at $\sim100$ GHz and combined them with existing VLA measurements at 6, 15 and 22 GHz. The resulting spectrum was fitted with the Granot--Sari broken power law in the slow-cooling regime using Bayesian nested sampling. The two-parameter model yields a peak frequency $\nu_m = 63^{+100}_{-33}$ GHz and a peak flux $F_m = 28\pm9$ $\mu$Jy. This solution is consistent with theoretical expectations for a young magnetar wind nebula, which predict a peak frequency in the same range and a self-absorption frequency below $\sim0.5$ GHz. With its inverted spectrum extending to tens of gigahertz and the highest peak frequency among known PRSs, the source associated with FRB20201124A is the most extreme in the current sample. Its properties match those expected for a rotation-powered magnetar wind nebula, making it a strong candidate for the youngest ($t\lesssim20$ yr) magnetar associated with a repeating FRB to date.

## 59. [Preparing for the Gaia DR4 Epochalypse: Simulated Epoch Astrometry for Substellar Companion Orbit-Modeling Benchmarks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06915) <a id="link59"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.06915

**Authors:** Daniel A. Yahalomi, Adrian M. Price-Whelan, Jessica Speedie, William Thompson, Megan Bedell, Victor Ramirez Delgado, Aida Behmard, Avraham Binnenfeld, Dori Blakely, Anthony G. A. Brown, Andrew R. Casey, Jiayin Dong, Clarissa R. Do Ó, Caleb Lammers, Cheyanne Shariat, Joshua N. Winn

**Abstract:** Gaia's fourth data release (DR4) will provide epoch astrometry for billions of sources, enabling the detection of potentially tens of thousands of substellar companions. We present the epochalypse catalog: Gaia DR4-like epoch astrometry for testing orbit-modeling pipelines on a common set of injected systems with known parameters. Onto each of the $5{,}040{,}328$ main-sequence Gaia stars within $500\,{ \rm pc}$ in the G23H catalog, we inject a zero-, one-, and two-companions system, for $15{,}120{,}984$ systems in total. Companion masses and semimajor axes are drawn log-uniformly over $1\,M_{ \rm Mars}$--$80\,M_{ \rm Jup}$ and $0.03$--$100\,{ \rm au}$, with no occurrence model and no detectability cut, so orbital periods extend well beyond the mission baseline and the companion-free population serves as a matched null. We also flag sub-populations of one- and two-companions systems whose companions are potentially detectable, based on an estimated signal-to-noise ratio that accounts for each star's noise, number of transits, and the suppression of signals with periods longer than the baseline. As a demonstration, we run the kepmodel astrometric periodogram on every system and find that signal detection and period localization decouple beyond the DR4 baseline: long-period companions can produce significant astrometric curvature while their periods, and therefore their masses, remain poorly constrained. Under any companion occurrence model, this population of systems where the orbit is detectable but not characterizable will far outnumber the population with fully characterizable orbits. The epochalypse catalog, its full generation pipeline, and a modeling tutorial are publicly available.

**Abstract:** We determine the convergence of quasi-normal modes sums for a point-particle plunging into a Schwarzschild black hole. While a dynamical amplitude model reproduces the waveform after the particle crosses the bounce radius, a constant amplitude sum converges only after the start time $t_S$, which corresponds to the advanced time at which the particle hits the central singularity. After $t_S$, dynamical and constant amplitudes models are equivalent; our analysis also implies that any "direct wave" must reduce to the late-time tail after $t_S$.

## 61. [Tracking turbulence for wind-driven halo removal using MagAO-X pyramid wavefront sensor images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06873) <a id="link61"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.06873

**Authors:** Jay K. Kueny, Jared R. Males, Alycia J. Weinberger, Laird M. Close, Joseph D. Long, Joshua Liberman, Sebastiaan Haffert, Eden McEwen, Maggie Y. Kautz, Olivier Guyon, Logan Pearce, Parker T. Johnson, Katie Twitchell, Jialin Li, Alex Hedglen, Avalon Gower, Warren Foster, Jhen Lumbres, Lauren Schatz

**Abstract:** The wind-driven halo (WDH) is a persistent, low spatial frequency noise artifact that arises due to the servo-lag error inherent to all adaptive optics (AO) instruments. Spatial filtering may be employed to overcome this artifact, however, filtering out the WDH while simultaneously preserving signal from an extended astrophysical object of interest is exceptionally challenging. Additionally, since the WDH changes in intensity and position angle through an observation, data-driven algorithms (e.g., KLIP) that are commonly used to subtract the starlight need to be overly-aggressive to remove both the static and dynamic noise components. Since wavefront sensors (WFSs) continuously track the closed-loop residual wavefront error, WFS telemetry presents the ideal resource for combating this type of noise artifact through postprocessing. Using archival WFS telemetry from MagAO-X, which is the "extreme" AO instrument for the 6.5-meter Magellan-Clay telescope, we demonstrate a novel workflow for WDH reconstruction and removal in individual coronagraphic science images. MagAO-X is equipped with a pyramid WFS capable of recording wavefront telemetry at a high-cadence which is saved during data acquisition. Given this, we detail how our WFS data processing pipeline, windsoCC, cross-correlates the recorded closed-loop wavefront to measure the wind vectors of several turbulent layers of the atmosphere above Las Campanas Observatory. We then make use of the wind parameters learned through windsoCC to reconstruct the WDH footprint by leveraging a parametric model. Notably, we demonstrate a dramatic improvement in object recovery using on-sky MagAO-X images of the disk around HR 4796A at visible wavelengths.

## 62. [Fast and accurate false-alarm probabilities for linear detection statistics in Poisson noise](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05510) <a id="link62"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.05510

**Authors:** Maayane T. Soumagnac, Eran O. Ofek, Shachar Israeli, David Glukhov

**Abstract:** Detecting weak signals in noisy data often requires calculating the statistical significance, or false- alarm probability, of a detection statistic. When the underlying noise follows Poisson statistics, as in low-count measurements such as photon-starved astronomical images, this calculation becomes non- trivial, and existing approaches typically rely on extensive simulations to estimate detection thresholds. We derive an accurate and efficient solution for computing false-alarm probabilities for arbitrary linear detection statistics under Poisson noise. We show that the detection statistic is a compound Poisson random variable, which unlocks efficient implementations based on the Fast Fourier Transform (FFT) and the Panjer recursion. The method is exact up to numerical discretization, accelerates computation by several orders of magnitude compared with simulations, and enables evaluation of false-alarm probabilities computationally inaccessible through simulation. Although motivated by low-count astronomical imaging, the framework applies broadly to detection problems involving Poisson-distributed events. It transforms the computation of detection significance under Poisson noise from a simulation problem into a deterministic numerical calculation. We provide open-source Python and MATLAB implementations.

**Novelty:** 5

**Abstract:** Little Red Dots (LRDs) are a population of compact, red sources discovered at high redshift with JWST that exhibit distinctive V-shaped spectral energy distributions (SEDs). Existing LRD samples are typically identified using a variety of color-based selection criteria, which can lead to differences in the populations recovered by different studies. We develop a cross-modal machine-learning framework that combines matched NIRCam imaging and NIRSpec/PRISM spectroscopy to transfer spectroscopic information into imaging-based representations, enabling LRD identification from JWST/NIRCam imaging alone. Applied to spectroscopically observed high-redshift galaxies previously classified as non-LRDs, our model identifies 35 new LRD candidates. The majority satisfy at least one independent LRD color selection criterion, and among the 13 candidates with medium- or high-resolution NIRSpec grating spectra, 10 exhibit broad H$\alpha$ emission - a hallmark of LRDs. We further apply the classifier to public NIRCam imaging from the Dawn JWST Archive and two independent JWST surveys, yielding 357 and 23 additional LRD candidates, respectively. The candidates recovered by our method span a broader range of observed SED properties than those in existing samples, suggesting that current color-selected samples may not fully capture the diversity of LRD-like systems present in JWST surveys. Our findings highlight the value of spectroscopy-informed photometric classification as a powerful strategy for systematically mining large-area JWST imaging datasets and provide a practical framework for future studies of this emerging population.

Our results demonstrate that imaging a large number of observations over a wide field area at sub-arcsecond resolution is now computationally feasible. Bye, Hugo Camacho, Cristina-Maria Cordun, Adam Fahs, Sonia Ghosh, Keith Goetz, Robert Grimm, Sven Herrmann, Joshua J. Hibbard, Oliver Jeong, Marc Klein-Wolt, Léon V. E. Koopmans, Joel Krajewski, Corentin K. Louis, Milan Maksimović, Ryan McLean, Raul A. Monsalve, Arnur Nigmetov, Paul O'Connor, Aaron Parsons, Michel Piat, Marc Pulupa, Rugved Pund, David Rapetti, Kaja M. Rotermund, Benjamin R. B. Saliwanchik, Jack Singal, Anže Slosar, Nikolai Stefanov, David Sundkvist, Aritoki Suzuki, Harish K. Vedantham, Philippe Zarka

**Abstract:** Carbon radio recombination lines (CRRLs) arising from the cold neutral medium (CNM) of the interstellar medium are potential foregrounds in the 1-50 MHz band that LuSEE-Night will observe from the lunar far side. CRRLs are sensitive probes of the temperature, density, and radiation field of the CNM, and LuSEE-Night will provide sky-averaged measurements in a poorly explored frequency range. We model CRRLs across this band, accounting for doppler, collisional, and radiation broadening, and we estimate line strengths using two independent methods - fixing either the peak or the frequency-integrated optical depth - applied both uniformly across the sky and with region-dependent values informed by prior low-frequency observations. We simulate LuSEE-Night's expected response to these CRRLs and further convolve the beam-diluted spectra with LuSEE-Night's polyphase-filter-bank spectrometer response, including its zoomed-mode frequency resolution of $\sim$390 Hz, to obtain the expected observed brightness temperature. Using these simulated spectra, we estimate the integration time required to detect individual CRRLs at SNR=5 across the six sky models considered. Required integration times range from as short as 30 hours (in the most optimistic model where the optical depth across the sky is $\sim$10$^{-3}$) to 27.6 years (in the most conservative models where the optical depth across the sky is $\sim$10$^{-4}$). Stacking the 361 simulated C$\alpha$ lines between 10 and 50 MHz reduces the integration time needed for an SNR=5 detection to $\sim$11 minutes and $\sim$151 hours respectively, indicating that LuSEE-Night has realistic chances of making a stacked detection of C$\alpha$ RRLs within a realistic mission lifetime, even under the most conservative optical-depth assumptions.

## 66. [Identifying Compact Object Candidates using Gaia DR3 Astrometric Accelerations and Jerks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05536) <a id="link66"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.05536

**Authors:** Andrew Kinnion, Jeff J. Andrews, Eirini Kasdagli

**Abstract:** Gaia Data Release 3 (DR3) provides astrometric measurements for more than a billion sources, hundreds of thousands of which have non-single-star astrometric solutions that indicate deviations from linear proper motion. Although sources with orbital solutions have been extensively inspected for compact objects via the astrometric mass function, two other solution types--the 7-parameter constant-acceleration solutions characterized by $\dot{\mathbf{\mu}}$, and the 9-parameter variable-acceleration solutions characterized by $\dot{\mathbf{\mu}}$ and $\ddot{\mathbf{\mu}}$--have not been as carefully explored. In this work, we develop a methodology for selecting sources within the Gaia DR3 9-parameter non-single-star astrometric solutions that are most likely to host massive compact companions. We assume that a source is the luminous star in a binary orbit around a dark object and derive analytic expressions for the sky-projected accelerations and jerks for three binary populations with different orbit approximations: inclined eccentric, face-on eccentric, and face-on circular. Using POSYDON, we develop a fourth, more realistic population of binaries, each consisting of a luminous star and a black hole. For each population, we address observational biases using gaiamock and select only the systems it fits with a 9-parameter solution. We find that binaries likely to host massive compact companions can be identified from the characteristic joint astrometric accelerations and jerks exhibited by the luminous stars, despite 9-parameter fits under-constraining full orbits. Finally, to illustrate the utility of our approach, we analyze a historical object of interest, HD 12871, and examine 9-parameter fits of Gaia BH3-like binaries.

## 67. [Primordial Black Holes and Induced Gravitational Waves from Inflation with History-Dependent Effective Dissipation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06329) <a id="link67"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.06329

**Authors:** Narges Rashidi

**Abstract:** We study inflation with an exponential factor multiplying the scalar Lagrangian and depending on the accumulated expansion history through a field-dependent coupling. Variation of the complete action on a finite interval yields an adjoint response with a final-time boundary condition. We derive the background and coupled scalar perturbation equations and examine five benchmarks for an exponential plateau potential and a smooth single-window coupling. The kinetic, direct-coupling and adjoint contributions nearly cancel, producing transient positive minima of the first Hubble slow-roll parameter, $\epsilon_1$, without a comparable reduction in field velocity. The scalar spectra, normalized to the cosmic microwave background (CMB) pivot amplitude, develop localized peaks with amplitudes $0.033$--$0.098$. Under Gaussian statistics and the adopted radiation-era collapse prescription, the the primordial black hole (PBH) abundance peaks span masses from $4.52\times10^{-13}$ to $34.05\,M_\odot$. The lightest-mass benchmark reaches $f_{\rm PBH}\simeq0.99$, allowing nearly all dark matter within the monochromatic approximation. The accompanying scalar-induced gravitational-wave spectra span peak frequencies from $6.78\times10^{-10}$ to $5.92\times10^{-3}\,\mathrm{Hz}$, with selected benchmarks lying within the projected sensitivities of future detectors.

**Novelty:** 5

## 68. [Gravitational waves flooding the Universe after wall domination](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05504) <a id="link68"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.05504

**Authors:** Sabir Ramazanov, Zhen-Min Zeng

**Abstract:** We discuss a possible early Universe scenario, where radiation era is interrupted by the epoch of domination of domain walls eventually annihilating into particles. During this period the Universe undergoes accelerated expansion, while the comoving correlation length of the domain wall network remains constant. The network serves as a source of tensor modes, which get frozen upon exiting the horizon similarly to the case of inflationary fluctuations. The modes re-enter the horizon during the subsequent stage of radiation domination and since then contribute to the gravitational wave background. The spectral shape of gravitational waves predicted in this scenario is markedly different compared to the case of domain walls remaining sub-dominant all the way till annihilation. These gravitational waves are strong enough to be observed with present-day and future facilities in a broad range of frequencies. In particular, their energy density is sufficient to produce a significant impact on the effective number of neutrino species, unless the duration of wall domination is suitably constrained.

## 69. [Adaptive Optics for the Extremely Large Telescope: overcoming Point Spread Function challenges with MORFEO](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06714) <a id="link69"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.06714

**Authors:** Carmelo Arcidiacono, Elisa Portaluri, Marco Gullieuszik, Michele Cantiello, Francesca Annibali, Guido Agapito, Demetrio Magrin, Vincenzo Cianniello, Paolo Ciliegi

**Abstract:** MORFEO is the multi-conjugate adaptive optics (MCAO) relay for the Extremely Large Telescope (ELT) of the European Southern Observatory. It combines the adaptive mirrors of the telescope (M4-M5) with two post-focal deformable mirrors to deliver a stable, diffraction-limited point spread function over a field approaching 1'. MORFEO senses the atmosphere using six sodium laser guide stars and up to three natural guide stars distributed across a $\sim$160'' technical field, enabling sky coverage levels of order 50% at the South Galactic Pole. Through its two gravity-invariant ports, MORFEO feeds the MICADO first-light imager and spectrograph with a corrected field of 51''and HARMONI, the high-angular resolution integral field spectrograph. SHARP, a concept for a multi-object near-infrared spectrograph at the ELT, adopts MORFEO as its reference MCAO architecture for evaluating achievable image quality and scientific performance. In this paper, we summarise the MCAO capabilities delivered by MORFEO, its optical quality, tomographic performance, stability of the corrected focal plane, and the accuracy of telemetry-based PSF reconstruction, and discuss their relevance for the development of SHARP and other wide-field spectroscopic concepts at the ELT.

## 70. [Magnetohydrodynamics During Reheating](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06872) <a id="link70"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.06872

**Authors:** Sovan Sau, Subhasis Maiti, Sahel Dey, Alberto Roper Pol, Debaprasad Maity

**Abstract:** Reheating is a crucial phase that connects inflation with the hot Big Bang Universe. During this phase, the nonlinear interactions among the inflaton, the radiation plasma, and the primordial magnetic fields can influence the subsequent evolution of cosmic structures we observe today. In this paper, we develop a multifluid framework to investigate the evolution of three dimensional nonlinear magnetohydrodynamics (MHD) during the non instantaneous reheating phase driven by the perturbative decay of the inflaton into the radiation plasma. For the present paper, we consider the simplest scenario where the inflaton field homogeneously injects energy into the radiation sector. We study the MHD evolution of the coupled inflaton radiation system during the reheating phase, including the evolution of their individual kinetic energies, the velocity perturbations, vorticity, magnetic fields, and gravitational waves. We find that homogeneous energy transfer from the inflaton to the radiation sector increases the radiation and kinetic energy densities but does not significantly amplify the initial magnetic field perturbations. Consequently, the magnetic spectrum remains largely unchanged with respect to the instantaneous reheating scenario, while the velocity perturbations undergo a scale redistribution that affects the resulting GW spectrum. Our results demonstrate the importance of nonlinear multifluid MHD dynamics in understanding the evolution of primordial fields during reheating and provide a framework for exploring more realistic scenarios involving inhomogeneous decay processes and detailed thermalization effects.

**Relevance:** 1
**Novelty:** 4

## 71. [PrimordialPy: A Python library for single-field inflation, primordial black holes, and scalar-induced gravitational waves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05566) <a id="link71"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.05566

**Authors:** Flavio Pineda

**Abstract:** We present \texttt{PrimordialPy}, an open-source Python library designed to compute the cosmological predictions of arbitrary canonical single-field inflationary models. By numerically solving the background dynamics and the curvature perturbation equation, the package evaluates the primordial power spectrum of scalar and tensor perturbations at all relevant scales. Furthermore, it automatically computes the resulting mass fraction and abundance of Primordial Black Holes (PBHs), as well as the associated spectrum of scalar-induced gravitational waves (SIGWs). Users can seamlessly implement any canonical single-field model by simply specifying the inflaton potential $V(\phi)$ and its parameters. Designed to be modular, efficient, and highly accessible, \texttt{PrimordialPy} comes with comprehensive documentation, validation benchmarks, and practical examples, making it a versatile tool for both research and education in early-universe cosmology.

## 72. [Can Magnetic Reconnection Accelerate Energetic Particles in the Heliosheath?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06735) <a id="link72"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.06735

**Authors:** Jeongbhin Seo, Teresa Boland, Fan Guo, Xiaocan Li, Giulia Murtas

**Abstract:** The mechanism for accelerating energetic particles in the outer heliosphere is still under debate. The termination shock, turbulence, and magnetic reconnection have all been proposed as viable mechanisms. We investigate compression-driven particle acceleration during magnetic reconnection in the heliospheric current sheet using the MHD--SDE framework that incorporates energetic-particle feedback into the MHD evolution. For the heliosheath conditions considered here, we find that reconnection-driven acceleration is inefficient downstream of the termination shock, whereas reconnection near the heliopause can generate stronger compression and produce a power-law differential intensity spectrum $J(\varepsilon)\propto\varepsilon^{-5.4}$. However, even the hardest spectrum achieved during magnetic reconnection remains much steeper than the typical $J(\varepsilon)\propto\varepsilon^{-1.5}$ spectrum observed in the heliosheath. This indicates that magnetic reconnection alone is insufficient to explain the acceleration of energetic particles in the heliosheath.

## 73. [Constraining the white-dwarf mass in the intermediate polar IGR J17014-4306 using X-ray and optical spectroscopy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06785) <a id="link73"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.06785

**Authors:** D. C. Souza, M. P. Diaz, A. S. Oliveira, G. J. M. Luna, C. V. Rodrigues, M. Reynolds, N. Palivanas, I. J. Lima, J. Miko{ł}ajewska, M. Orio

**Abstract:** Intermediate polars (IPs), a subclass of magnetic cataclysmic variables, often show discrepancies between white-dwarf (WD) mass measurements from X-ray spectral modeling and optical dynamical studies. We investigate the IP IGR J17014-4306. We performed a joint spectral analysis of NuSTAR observations combined with archival XMM-Newton and Swift data, modeling the broad-band X-ray spectrum with a multi-temperature cooling-flow model. Additionally, we obtained time-resolved optical spectroscopy with the SOAR telescope to measure the radial velocity of the donor star and constrain the system's orbital parameters. The X-ray spectral analysis reveals a very high post-shock plasma temperature of $kT_{\max}=50_{-8}^{+11}\,\mathrm{keV}$, corresponding to a WD mass of $M_{\mathrm{WD}}=0.94_{-0.07}^{+0.09}\,M_\odot$. We also fitted the NuSTAR spectrum above 10 keV with the ipolar30KK model, obtaining $M_{\mathrm{WD}}=0.88_{-0.12}^{+0.14}\,M_\odot$. Our optical spectroscopy yields a radial-velocity semi-amplitude for the companion star of $K_2=165\pm4\,\mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$ and an optical mass function of $f(M)=0.249\pm0.018\,M_\odot$. Combined with the eclipse geometry and the WD mass estimate from the X-ray models, these results favor a high mass ratio, $0.76\lesssim q\lesssim0.95$, and a donor-star mass of approximately $0.67\lesssim M_2/M_\odot\lesssim0.89$. In the limiting case of $q=1$, the WD mass has an upper limit of $1.14\,M_\odot$. Together with the X-ray measurements, this indicates a relatively massive WD. The lack of an independent measurement of the WD dynamical mass leaves it unclear whether the dynamical mass is consistent with the X-ray measurements or whether the X-ray mass is underestimated, as has been found for other IPs. Thus, our constraints make IGR J17014-4306 an important system for improving our understanding of IPs.

## 74. [APOLLO-Timing: Annealed Phase - Orbit Lattice Linking across Observations for Binary Pulsar Timing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06640) <a id="link74"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.06640

**Authors:** Y. R. Wen, D. Zhao, N. Wang, J. P. Yuan, W. M. Yan, J. B. Wang, J. T. Bai, Z. H. Chen

**Abstract:** Sparse observations of binary pulsars can leave the orbit and the pulse count across observing gaps uncertain. We present APOLLO-Timing, a Bayesian phase-orbit lattice method that fits timing parameters together with integer pulse offsets between observing sessions. PINT checks the candidate connections at fixed pulse numbers. For PSR J1912-1215, a preliminary Keplerian fit to variations in the apparent spin period and its derivative did not phase-connect 96 times of arrival (TOAs) spanning 1438.07 days in 23 sessions. Starting from its position, spin frequency F0, and first time derivative F1 estimated in that fit, with dispersion measure fixed, APOLLO-Timing recovers the orbit and a unique best-supported phase-connected solution without supplied orbital parameters. A repeat full-data blind search recovers the same pulse counts in 914.0 s, including the final fit. Setting the initial F1 to zero in the 17, 18, and 19 session subsets also recovers the reference path. The solution gives Pb = 17.526 days, x = 23.448 light-seconds, e = 2.65e-5, chi-square = 188.01, and a weighted rms residual of 5.08 microseconds. For a pulsar mass of 1.4 solar masses and inclination of 60 degrees, the inferred companion mass is 0.67 solar masses, favoring a CO/ONeMg white dwarf.

## 75. [Revisiting the Stellar-Dynamical Mass of the Central Black Hole in NGC 3227](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05547) <a id="link75"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.05547

**Authors:** Nabanita Das, Misty C. Bentz, Eugene Vasiliev, Monica Valluri, Christopher A. Onken

**Abstract:** We present a new stellar-dynamical mass for the supermassive black hole in the nearby Seyfert galaxy NGC 3227. We determined the line-of-sight velocity distributions of stars in the nuclear region of NGC 3227 using archival spatially-resolved $K$-band spectra obtained with NIFS at Gemini Observatory. The surface brightness profile of the galaxy is described by four components, measured from a WFC3 F547M image from the Hubble Space Telescope. We constructed dynamical models using the Schwarzschild orbit-superposition method implemented in the open-source code $\texttt{FORSTAND}$. We find a best-fit black hole mass of $M_{\mathrm{BH}}$=$(1.2\pm0.5) \times 10^7$ $M_{\odot}$. This mass is consistent with a previous mass from R. I. Davies et al. based on stellar dynamics using orbit-based modeling, but our results make use of higher signal-to-noise data from which the higher-order moments of the stellar LOSVDs are constrained, thus providing a more precise $M_{\mathrm{BH}}$. Our result is also consistent with the $M_{\mathrm{BH}}$ constraint from velocity-resolved reverberation mapping reported by M. C. Bentz et al.. And while our best-fit mass is slightly smaller than the mass constraint from gas-dynamical modeling reported by E. K. S. Hicks & M. A. Malkan (after scaling to the same adopted distance), they are within 2$\sigma$ of each other. Direct comparisons of black hole masses from different techniques are rare but crucial to ensure all black hole masses are on the same scale, and to identify and minimize the uncertainties and possible sources of bias associated with each technique.

U. Shivashankara, G. Sigl, K. Simkova, F. Simon, R. Šmída, S. Soares Sippert, P. Sommers, S. Stanič, J. Stasielak, P. Stassi, S. Strähnz, M. Straub, T. Suomijärvi, A. D. Supanitsky, Z. Svozilikova, Z. Szadkowski, F. Tairli, A. Tapia, C. Taricco, C. Timmermans, O. Tkachenko, P. Tobiska, C. J. Todero Peixoto, B. Tomé, A. Travaini, P. Travnicek, C. Trimarelli, M. Tueros, M. Unger, R. Verzi, J. Vicha, L. M. A. Victoria, S. Vorobiov, J. B. Vuta, A. A. Watson, A. Weindl, M. Weitz, L. Wiencke, H. Wilczyński, B. Wundheiler, B. Yue, A. Yushkov, E. Zas, D. Zavrtanik, M. Zavrtanik

**Abstract:** We investigate the sensitivity of the Pierre Auger Observatory to sterile neutrinos with masses in the MeV-to-100 MeV range. To this end, we consider a minimal $3+1$ framework in which a single sterile state mixes mainly with the $\nu_\tau$ flavor through an active--sterile mixing angle $\theta_N$ to produce a mass eigenstate $N_1$. At ultrahigh energies, $N_1$ neutrinos can traverse substantial chord lengths through the Earth and interact through their active component, producing $\tau$ leptons that may emerge near the surface and decay in the atmosphere, thereby generating detectable air showers. We compute the corresponding exposures with a detailed Monte Carlo simulation of neutrino propagation, interactions, $\tau$ energy losses and decay, and derive upper limits at $90\%$ confidence level on an isotropic diffuse flux of $N_1$ neutrinos. For an $E^{-2}$ spectrum, we obtain $E^2\Phi_N\leq 2\times10^{-9}\sin^{-2}\theta_N\,\mathrm{GeV\,cm^{-2}\,sr^{-1}\,s^{-1}}$ over $0.3$--$200$ EeV for $\sin^2\theta_N\lesssim10^{-1}$. We further consider $N_1$ neutrinos arising from the decay of a superheavy dark-matter particle. For a benchmark dark-matter mass of $10^{13}$ GeV, we derive constraints on the active--sterile mixing angle and dark-matter lifetime for sterile-neutrino masses of $10$ and $100$ MeV. These results illustrate the potential of the Observatory to probe long-lived weakly interacting particles.

## 77. [Developing the Field Selector System for the SHARP Multi-IFU: Optomechanical Design and First Prototype Validation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06332) <a id="link77"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.06332

**Authors:** H. Mahmoodzadeh, P. Saracco, I. Di Antonio, P. Conconi, D. Scaccabarozzi, B. Saggin, E. Molinari, C. Arcidiacono, E. Cascone, V. Cianniello, F. D'Alessio, V. De Caprio, G. Di Rico, B. Di Francesco, C. Eredia, P. Franzetti, M. Fumana, E. Portaluri, F. Vitali

**Abstract:** This paper presents the innovative Field Selector System (FSS) developed for VESPER, the multi-Integral Field Unit of the SHARP near-infrared spectrograph. The proposed system employs an array of movable light-collection probes (Field Selectors) that travel along parallel, vertically offset planes to avoid mechanical interference and vignetting. The true novelty lies in its opto-mechanical synchronization: as each probe translates by {\Delta}y to select a target, a set of folding mirrors shifts by {\Delta}y/2. This maintains a constant optical path length, allowing multiple independently moving probes to share a single focal plane and form a continuous image on it, easy to slice by the slicer. A dedicated optical test setup was developed to characterize the positioning stability and repeatability of the probe motion. The measured mechanical errors were converted into equivalent optical tilts and incorporated into the SHARP optical model to assess their impact on instrument performance. The experimental results identified the dominant sources of positioning error and provided valuable insight into the relationship between mechanical tolerances and optical performance. The study also highlights several design improvements capable of significantly reducing probe tilting and enhancing system stability. The results demonstrate the viability of the proposed concept and indicate that, with the identified improvements, the system represents an excellent field-selection solution for SHARP and other multi-integral-field-unit (mIFU) spectrographs.

**Relevance:** 1
**Novelty:** 4

## 78. [The spatial structure of solar magnetoconvection at scales up to $60$~Mm](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05674) <a id="link78"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.05674

**Authors:** D. Przybylski, R. H. Cameron

**Abstract:** While convective motions at the scale of granules on the solar surface are well understood, those of mesogranular and supergranulation are not. At still larger scales, the lack of giant cells is a major mystery. We aim to slightly improve understanding of the radial and horizontal flows associated at mesogranular scales and supergranulation. We perform a deep MURaM small-scale dynamo simulation, extending from $50$~Mm below the solar surface and with a horizontal size of 60~Mm and sufficient resolution to begin to properly resolve the magnetic bright points in intergranular lanes. We determine the structure of the convective modes from the simulations at different scales, and compare the power spectra to those which have been previously observed. At all scales which are resolved by the simulation the simulated flows agree with the amplitude implied by Doppler measurements and local correlation tracking (LCT) measurements. The power in the vertical Doppler velocities at supergranular and larger scales is shown to be influenced by density perturbations near the surface. The structure of the modes at depth is simple as expected from the previous study of \cite{LordCameron2014} except the horizontal forcing scale scales like $H_\rho^{2/3}$ instead of $H\rho$. The simulations reproduce the observations up to scales of $60$~Mm. Within the simulated range, $l>=70$, we do not see the excess power associated with the convective conundrum.

## 79. [Tiny Quiet-Sun EUV Ejections Revealed by High-resolution Multiwavelength Observations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05934) <a id="link79"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.05934

**Authors:** Nengyi Huang, Yuqian Wei, Qin Li, Jeongwoo Lee, Haimin Wang

**Abstract:** We investigate two successive tiny quiet-Sun EUV ejections on 2022 October 29 using coordinated Solar Orbiter/EUI, IRIS, SDO/AIA, and Goode Solar Telescope observations. We examine their low-lying geometry and the chromospheric and transition-region response before and between the releases. A curved H-alpha thread is present beforehand, and Mg ii and Si iv spectra reveal a compact multithermal precursor 2.6--2.7 min before the first EUV brightening. EUI--AIA stereoscopy places the recurrent compact EUV kernel near 2.2 Mm and the sampled emission at approximately 0.6--4.2 Mm along inclined, locally arched structures. The first ejection shows multithreaded, helical-looking EUV strands; the second shows straighter strands and a decrease in the cross-ridge separation of redshifted and blueshifted Mg ii k3 samples from 1.47'' to 0.89''. Nevertheless, the two peaks have comparable emission-measure increments and estimated event-associated thermal contents of a few 10^26 erg under the same assumptions. Before the second ejection, the regional Si iv and k3 blueshift signals remain enhanced, while the Mg ii central reversal along the activated ridge remains shallower than before the event. Component reconnection between the curved structure and the surrounding field offers a possible explanation. The observations show that jet-like EUV emission can occur in low-lying structures. The second ejection begins before the chromospheric and transition-region spectra have returned to their pre-event states. Its spectral signatures may therefore depend on the atmospheric changes associated with the first ejection as well as on the additional energy input.

## 80. [Longitudinal-field particle acceleration in channels generated by strong electromagnetic waves in pair plasma](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05948) <a id="link80"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.05948

**Authors:** Kavin Tangtartharakul, Andrei M. Beloborodov, Carolina Herrera Hernandez, Mihail O. Cernaianu, Alexey Arefiev

**Abstract:** Relativistically strong electromagnetic waves interacting with pair plasmas appear in astrophysical settings and may become accessible in future laboratory experiments using high-power lasers. Transverse modulations and filamentation can turn extended wavefronts into structures of a finite transverse width. We examine the interaction of such a finite-width wave incident on a cold, uniform pair plasma using a three-dimensional kinetic particle-in-cell simulation. The wave does not penetrate the plasma and instead advances by creating a fully evacuated channel. The wave loses energy at a rate that cannot be explained by plasma clearing alone. We find that the excess losses are caused by strong particle acceleration near the walls of the channel. The acceleration is driven by a longitudinal electric field associated with the transverse wave structure and the geometry of the self-generated channel.

## 81. [HLA-Net: A Physics-Guided Skeleton-Based Framework for Automatic Gravitational Arc Detection in Astronomical Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05438) <a id="link81"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.05438

**Authors:** Don S

**Abstract:** Gravitational arcs produced by strong gravitational lensing provide a unique approach for investigating dark matter distribution, galaxy cluster mass reconstruction and the structure of the Universe. Automated detection remains challenging because gravitational arcs are rare, often faint and morphologically similar to elongated galaxies.In this paper, we propose \textbf{HLA-Net (Hybrid Lens Arc Network)}, an interpretable multi-stage framework for automatic gravitational arc detection that explicitly exploits both the geometric morphology and physical characteristics of gravitational lensing without requiring manually annotated training datasets.The proposed framework was evaluated on astronomical FITS images containing complex galaxy cluster structures. Starting from 169 initial arc candidates extracted from ordered skeleton representations, the multi-stage verification pipeline reduced the search space to 83 geometrically consistent candidates, while robust circle fitting successfully modelled 113 candidate arc structures. The results demonstrate that HLA-Net effectively suppresses false-positive detections while preserving physically reasonable lens candidates through an interpretable and modular verification strategy.

## 82. [A Laplace-Transform Green's Function for Two-Zone Diffusion with Energy Losses: Application to the Geminga Pulsar Halo](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05808) <a id="link82"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.05808

**Authors:** Furong Li, Wei Liu, Yali Shao, Yiqing Guo

**Abstract:** Extended TeV gamma-ray halos around pulsars are commonly interpreted as evidence for slow diffusion of high-energy electrons and positrons near their sources, with diffusion coefficients about two orders of magnitude below the Galactic average inferred from B/C measurements. A two-zone diffusion picture offers a natural phenomenological resolution: particles are temporarily confined in a slow-diffusion region around the source and subsequently propagate through the outer region with a diffusion coefficient close to the Galactic average. Using a Laplace-transform method, we derive an analytical Green's-function solution to the spherically symmetric two-zone diffusion equation, in which the diffusion coefficient changes discontinuously at r_b. The solution satisfies continuity of both the particle density and the diffusive flux at the boundary and reduces to the standard Gaussian kernel in the one-zone limit. For the parameter set used in our comparison, the spatial kernel and the positron flux at Earth agree numerically with an independent integral solution of the same two-zone problem. Applied to Geminga, the two-zone models examined here produce a more extended outer particle distribution and broader positron spectra than the homogeneous diffusion model, with higher positron fluxes in the TeV range. The adopted injection model further affects the height and shape of the high-energy spectral peak. The predicted gamma-ray surface-brightness profile also reproduces the main radial decline observed by HAWC.

## 83. [Energetic Nonthermal Electrons within the Above-the-looptop Regions in Solar Flares: Acceleration, Feedback, and Quasi-Periodic Pulsations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06763) <a id="link83"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.06763

**Authors:** Jeongbhin Seo, Fan Guo, Xiaocan Li, Bin Chen, Chengcai Shen, Hui Li

**Abstract:** Solar flares are among the most dramatic events in the solar system, releasing substantial magnetic energy and accelerating a large number of electrons to high energies. Notably, in certain events, the above-the-looptop region may contain a significant population of nonthermal electrons, both in number and energy. For the first time, we adopt a novel numerical method that combines magnetohydrodynamics with energetic particles incorporating feedback from nonthermal electrons to investigate electron acceleration and transport in solar flares. We find that a large fraction of energetic electrons are accelerated via the current sheet and termination shock regions. Most energetic electrons are concentrated in the above-the-looptop region, carrying a sizable amount of the released energy.   We observe that greater feedback of nonthermal electrons leads to steeper energy spectra. The energy density of the nonthermal electrons oscillates due to the periodic impact of magnetic islands into the above-the-looptop, which may help explain the observed quasi-periodic pulsations (QPPs). Our simulations provide new insights into the origin of nonthermal electrons and associated emissions in the above-the-looptop region.

## 84. [The PAU Survey: Self-supervised image denoising for flux-preserving photometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05447) <a id="link84"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.05447

**Authors:** J. Chen, M. Eriksen, D. Navarro-Gironés, P. Renard, E. Gaztanaga, J. Carretero, R. Casas, F. J. Castander, E. Fernandez, J. García-Bellido, R. Miquel, C. Padilla, E. Sánchez, I. Sevilla-Noarbe, P. Tallada-Crespí

**Abstract:** Context. Deep photometric surveys increasingly rely on low signal-to-noise imaging, where noise affects source detection, flux measurements, photometric redshifts, and sample selection. Self-supervised denoising methods are attractive because they do not require ground-truth images, but applying them to quantitative photometry requires controlling the flux biases they may introduce. Aims. We evaluate and improve Noise2Void (N2V) for denoising real astronomical images, focusing on preserving fluxes, reducing photometric scatter, and retaining the main source features across a wide magnitude range. Methods. We use real observations from the Physics of the Accelerating Universe Survey (PAUS) together with PAUS-like simulated images. We show that standard N2V systematically underestimates galaxy fluxes, especially for faint galaxies. To mitigate this bias, we add a bounded-output layer that limits large negative outputs, and fine-tune the model with low-rank adaptation (LoRA) and two flux-aware loss terms that preserve the flux scale and improve repeated-exposure flux consistency. Results. For PAUS galaxies with 22 <= i_AB < 23 in the NB645 band, the standard N2V flux-ratio distribution peaks far below unity, while the bounded-output model restores it close to the original PAUS flux scale. The flux-aware LoRA fine-tuning reduces the width of the repeated-exposure flux-difference distribution to sigma_68 = 0.529 in NB645, an effective 47% reduction in random flux scatter relative to the original PAUS uncertainty scale, while keeping the median flux ratio close to unity. Conclusions. Denoising methods for astronomical surveys must be evaluated with photometric diagnostics, not image-quality metrics alone. With flux-aware modifications, self-supervised denoising can be adapted to low-SNR survey images while preserving the flux information needed for quantitative analysis.

## 85. [Collective Characterization of Polar Crown Filament Eruptions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05902) <a id="link85"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.05902

**Authors:** Ying Wang Yan Xu, Ju Jing, Vasyl Yurchyshyn, Haimin Wang

**Abstract:** Polar crown filaments (PCFs) are crucially related to the structure of solar polar magnetic fields. They are physically significant because of the ``rush-to-the-pole'' motion, which is an indicator of the poleward drift of magnetic fields from decayed active regions. The formation and evolution of PCFs offer important insights into the Sun's magnetic activity at high latitudes and the solar cycle variation. In this study, we present a collective analysis of 50 PCF disappearance events covering solar cycle 24 and 25. Among them, 24 show rotational motions, and nine have sufficiently clear magnetic configurations for comparison with numerical model predictions. In all nine events, the observed rotation directions agree with the model predictions, suggesting possible magnetic-flux-rope-like structures in these events. All except one of the PCFs in our sample disappeared through eruption rather than thermal disappearance, and most associated CMEs were slow ($<800$ km s$^{-1}$). For events accompanied by transient coronal holes, the estimated magnetic flux associated with the dimming regions is on the order of $10^{20}$--$10^{21}$ Mx. Time--distance analysis identifies 22 events with a clear slow-rise to fast-rise transition, with onset heights ranging from several tens to about 160 Mm and a mean onset velocity of about 2.8 km s$^{-1}$ after excluding the highest-velocity event. Together, these findings characterize the magnetic structure and dynamics of PCF eruptions.

## 86. [Insights on the mass composition of ultrahigh-energy cosmic rays from small-scale anisotropy with large-scale structure simulations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05783) <a id="link86"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.05783

**Authors:** Ryo Higuchi, Eiji Kido, Shigehiro Nagataki

**Abstract:** Ultra-high-energy cosmic rays (UHECRs) above 100 EeV provide important clues to the most energetic processes in the Universe. At the highest energies, it is expected that UHECRs originate only from nearby sources because of energy losses during propagation. In such a regime, small-scale anisotropies in arrival directions could appear if the magnetic deflections are sufficiently small, for example, for a limited fraction of UHECRs with relatively low electric charge. Using the Pierre Auger Observatory open data, we confirm there is no significant small-scale anisotropy above 100 EeV within 3 degrees, indicating an almost isotropic sky distribution. To interpret this, we consider that heavier nuclei have higher photodisintegration thresholds due to their smaller Lorentz factors, resulting in longer propagation lengths. Combination with the nearly homogeneous distribution of galaxies at such distances can explain the absence of anisotropies above 100 EeV. Based on this idea, we perform mock observations of UHECRs above 100 EeV assuming pure-proton, iron, and uranium compositions. We predict the expected UHECR distributions using the Millennium Run semi-analytic galaxy catalog and turbulent extragalactic magnetic fields. By varying the source density and magnetic field strength, we evaluate the anisotropy for each case. Without magnetic fields, the observed isotropy above 100 EeV excludes the pure-proton and pure-iron cases. The pure-uranium case can reproduce the observed isotropy when the source density is high and the magnetic field is turbulent. When the turbulent field is strong, both the iron and uranium cases remain consistent with the data, whereas almost no parameter set can explain the observation for a pure-proton composition. Our results constrain the possible combinations of source density and magnetic field strength across different UHECR compositions.

## 87. [From Earth's Harshest Landscapes to Lunar Horizons: Engineering NEA Telescopes for Extreme Environments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05435) <a id="link87"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.05435

**Authors:** M. Hadjara, A. Pomazan, H. Ji, Q. Dong, J. Zheng, M. Yindun, L. Cament, M. Adams, M. Diaz, R. Mendez, B. Foing, M. Meftah, Z. Ping, R. Ikhlef, D. Baba Aissa

**Abstract:** Near-Earth Asteroids (NEAs) represent both a scientific opportunity and a potential threat to human civilization. While current survey programs have successfully detected most large NEAs, a significant observational gap remains for objects smaller than approximately 140 m, which can still produce regional-scale devastation. Ground-based surveys are intrinsically limited by atmospheric conditions, weather, and solar avoidance constraints, which leave a significant fraction of near-Sun objects difficult to detect. In particular, Earth-based observatories remain largely blind to objects approaching from the Sun-ward direction, reducing available warning times. Future lunar observatories offer a promising solution to this limitation. A telescope operating on the Moon could enable observations of regions inaccessible from Earth, provide enhanced sensitivity, and significantly improve early detection capabilities. However, the lunar environment imposes severe engineering constraints, including thermal cycling, dust contamination, radiation exposure, and autonomous operation requirements. We propose the AMAS(Atacama-Mongolia-Antarctica-Sahara) project, an international initiative using Earth's most extreme environments as analogue sites for future lunar astronomical facilities. By deploying and testing specialized NEA survey telescopes in these environments, key technologies for future lunar observatories can be validated under realistic operational conditions. This paper presents the scientific rationale, engineering challenges, site selection strategy, and long-term roadmap toward a global Earth-Moon network for planetary defense.

## 88. [One gravitational function across matter phases: compatibility and interface constraints in static $f(R,T)$ gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05657) <a id="link88"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.05657

**Authors:** Samuel Isidoro dos Santos Júnior, Rafael C. R. de Lima

**Abstract:** Conservative $f(R,T)=R+h(T)$ gravity fixes $h$ by demanding that the matter stress tensor be conserved, and the reconstruction is normally carried out for one equation of state at a time. We ask whether one function $h(T)$ can serve two matter phases separated by a sharp transition. The analysis is static and local: spherically symmetric backgrounds, the $\mathcal{L}_m=\rho$ closure, cold barotropic perfect fluids, and a Maxwell interface without a surface layer. A common $h$ must satisfy two conditions that single-phase reconstructions do not impose. It must solve both reconstruction equations at every trace value occupied by both phases, and it must make the effective normal pressure continuous across the interface; equality of material pressure and chemical potential does not secure the latter. For a hadronic polytrope ($K>0$, $\Gamma>1$) and an affine quark equation of state, an open overlap of the occupied trace ranges forces $h$ to be constant on both. When the ranges are separated, both conditions can be met by a nonconstant $C^2$ function, which we construct explicitly at a single interface. Two further results bound this local construction. If the same polytrope extends to zero density, regularity of $h$ at $T=0$ removes its nonconstant part there; and wherever $h'(T)\neq0$, separate conservation forbids any change in the density of a fluid element.

**Abstract:** A comprehensive photometric and spectroscopic analysis of TIC 349005337, a highly active M-type detached eclipsing binary with an orbital period of 2.6079133 days, is presented utilizing high-cadence and long-term monitoring photometry from the Transiting Exoplanet Survey Satellite (TESS) and medium-resolution spectroscopy from the Large Sky Area Multi-Object Fiber Spectroscopic Telescope (LAMOST). The frequent superflares suggest that intense magnetic activity and significant starspot coverage play a dominant role in altering the stellar atmospheric structure. As the secondary component falls below the fully convective boundary, these energetic flares provide critical empirical constraints for distributed dynamo models in the low-mass regime. Our results highlight TIC 349005337 as an ideal laboratory for investigating the interplay between binary dynamics, magnetic reconnection, and the resulting impact on the atmospheric stability of potential orbiting exoplanets.

The 2025 DM time series is distinguished from those of the other years by a secondary enhancement on 21 June, about one week after the main conjunction peak. In our scattering-corrected DM measurements, this feature, $\Delta$DM $= (3.95 \pm 0.20) \times 10^{-3}$ pc cm$^{-3}$, is about 20 times the day-to-day DM scatter outside the conjunction window. The RM time series, measured by combining the single pulses of each observation and corrected for the ionospheric contribution, show conjunction excursions of up to $\sim 60$ rad m$^{-2}$ and changes of sign consistent with crossings of heliospheric magnetic-sector boundaries, but no counterpart to the secondary 2025 DM feature or to a similar post-conjunction DM tail in 2026. From the slope of the RM excess against the DM excess we estimate the electron-density-weighted line-of-sight magnetic field; for the sectors with significant slopes, $|\langle B_\parallel \rangle|$ is at least $\sim 0.2$ to $\sim 10$ mG near conjunction, largest in 2025.

## 91. [On the Detectability of Compact Multi-Planet Systems via Gravitational Microlensing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05535) <a id="link91"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.05535

**Authors:** Stephen R. Kane

**Abstract:** Compact multi-planet systems orbiting ultracool dwarfs, such as TRAPPIST-1, may be a major population of terrestrial worlds with poorly constrained demographics. Individual close-in planets are expected to escape microlensing detection, but whether a compact system can produce a detectable collective signal remains to be quantified. We calculate the microlensing signal from each TRAPPIST-1 planet, and of all seven combined, if the system were a lens toward the bulge. For a 0.09 $M_\odot$ lens at 4 kpc, $R_E \approx 1.21$ AU, placing the planets at $s = 0.010$--$0.051~\theta_E$, 12--63 times interior to the lensing zone. We measure each signal as the residual after fitting a single-lens model, which accounts for the barycentric dipole. Because the quadrupole term cancels between the two images to first order, the residual scales as the octupole ($q s^3$), reaching $|\delta A / A| \sim 10^{-6}$. The seven planets combined yield $\Delta\chi^2 \sim 10^{-3}$ even at 1~mmag precision, five orders of magnitude below the standard detection threshold. Detection requires increasing all planet masses by a factor of $\sim$300, whereas the resonant chain loses its guarantee of Hill stability at a factor of $\sim$7. Only a lens within $\sim$15 pc of the source or the Sun places the outer planets in the lensing zone. The Nancy Grace Roman Space Telescope is therefore not expected to detect a TRAPPIST-1-like system through microlensing, whereas its transit channel in the same fields can detect such planets in large numbers, marking a sharp boundary in the Galactic census between microlensing and transit sensitivity.

## 92. [Aperture-dependent [O III] profile variability: Clues to the nature of double-peaked narrow emission lines](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06150) <a id="link92"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.06150

**Authors:** Qi Zheng, Xingqian Chen, Zhegeng Chen, Xue-Guang Zhang, Qirong Yuan

**Abstract:** We propose a simple and practical aperture-dependent method for investigating the physical origin of double-peaked narrow emission lines (DPNELs). Spectroscopic observations obtained from different apertures probe different spatial regions and therefore different gas kinematics within the narrow line region (NLR). Consequently, changes in the profiles of DPNELs observed with different apertures can provide important constraints on their physical origins. We applied this method to SDSS J0941 by comparing spectra obtained from the SDSS, DESI, and LAMOST surveys. Significant differences are found in the double-peaked profiles of the different spectra, demonstrating that aperture effects can strongly influence the integrated DPNEL profiles. The AGN model, where the two nuclei lie in a plane perpendicular to the line of sight, can qualitatively explain the asymmetric sampling of the two NLRs, but it cannot explain the different peak separations of different spectra. The observed asymmetric evolution of the two components disfavors a simple AGN-driven outflow and a rotating disk-like NLR scenario. Of the models considered, only a more complex non-axisymmetric rotating NLR provides a self-consistent explanation for the observed aperture-dependent variations. In this scenario, the blueshifted emitting region is more compact and remains largely enclosed by the smaller aperture, resulting in an increased line width with little change in flux. In contrast, the redshifted emitting region is more spatially extended and is therefore more strongly affected by the reduced aperture, leading to pronounced decreases in both flux and line width. Our results illustrate the diagnostic potential of combining multi-aperture spectroscopic surveys to study DPNEL origins, while highlighting the difficulty of distinguishing between physical scenarios.

The SO shows quiescent-to-peak amplitude of 4.97 +/- 0.18 mag and duration of 16.59 days which are typical for the SU UMa-type DNe. Using the superhump period-orbital period relation of SU UMa stars, we obtain the approximate distance of 3.7 +/- 0.3 kpc to KSP-OT-201803a and Galactocentric distance of 9.4 kpc and height from the Galactic plane of +1.2 kpc. Stars at these large distances may belong to the Galactic halo or thick disk, which contain old, metal-poor Population II stars; therefore, these systems may have undergone a different evolutionary process than Population I objects. The orbital period gap of DNe spans 2.45-3.18 hours. Assuming that the orbital period of KSP-OT-201803a is a few percent shorter than its superhump period, KSP-OT-201803a may have just emerged from the period gap and recently resumed mass transfer after crossing its lower boundary.
